みずほ経済 EXPRESS

みずほ GDP ナウ（2026 年 10 月中旬時点） 
～7～9 月期 GDP は前期比＋0.03％（年率＋0.10％）と推計～

調査部  シニア日本経済エコノミスト 越山祐資 
同             エコノミスト  阿部大樹 
080-1069-4835 yusuke.koshiyama@mizuho-rt.co.jp 
■ 10 月中旬時点の「みずほ GDP ナウ」は 7～9 月期 GDP を前期比＋0.10％と予測 
みずほ総合研究所では景気動向をタイムリーに把握するべく、浦沢（2023）等を参考にGDPナウキ
ャスティングに取り組んできた。これを「みずほGDPナウ」と呼称することとし、太田他（2024）で
はデータドリブン予測である「みずほGDPナウ」が民間予測平均並みの予測精度を確保できることを
示した。その上で、酒井他（2024）をはじめとして、月次経済指標を用いたGDPナウキャスティング
の結果を紹介してきたところである。 
本稿では10月中旬時点までに得られる月次経済指標を用い、 「みずほGDPナウ」 による7～9月期GDP
の予測結果を紹介する。予測に用いたデータは、8月分の鉱工業生産、 消費活動指数、 所定外労働時間、
消費財出荷指数、第3次産業活動指数、9月分の中小企業景況調査（売上げ見通しDI）である1。図表1
には、予測に使用した各月次経済指標の推移を示している。 
図表 1 各月次経済指標の推移

（注）青字は改善、赤字は悪化を示す

日本銀行「消費活動指数」より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

### Page 2

2

モデルによる10月中旬時点における7 ～9月期実質GDP成長率の予測値は、図表2のとおり前期比＋
0.03％ （年率＋0.10％）となった。 他機関による予測では、東京財団政策研究所による10月15日時点の
ナウキャスティングが同＋0.29％（年率＋1.18％）、日本経済研究センターが公表した10月のESPフォ
ーキャスト調査（回答期間：2026年9月30日～10月7日、回答者37名）における民間予測値平均が前期
比年率＋0.08％となっている。「みずほGDPナウ」の予測値は、民間コンセンサスよりやや高いもの
の、概ね近い伸びを予測していると言える。 
なお、多くの民間エコノミストが保守的な見方をしている理由の一つには、GDP統計作成上のテク
ニカルな要因がある。4～6月期には、 中東情勢悪化を背景に原油 ・ ナフサ輸入が大幅に減少したほか、
石油化学製品の供給途絶懸念から幅広い企業で在庫積み増しの動きが生じた。やや直感に反するが、
いずれもGDPに対しては押し上げ要因となる。 そのため、4～6月期GDPは前期比＋0.36％ （年率＋1.45％）
とプラス成長になったものの、 成長率が示すほど景気の実態は強くなかったとみるのが自然であろう。
反対に、7～9月期については、4～6月期の反動で輸入増・在庫減の動きが顕在化し、GDPには一定の
下方圧力がかかる可能性がある。みずほ総合研究所が9月に公表したレポート2でも、こうしたテクニ
カル要因を考慮の上、7～9月期GDPは横ばい圏の伸びにとどまると予想している。 
■ 「みずほ GDP ナウ」による予測値下方修正の主因は生産・消費関連指標の悪化 
「みずほGDPナウ」 による7～9月期のGDP（1次速報値） の予測にあたっては10月中旬までに公表さ
れた月次経済指標を使って予測値を4回更新した。図表3では、予測値の更新過程と各月次経済指標の
寄与度を示している3。予測値が下方修正された主因は、8月の鉱工業生産、 消費財出荷指数の低下だ。
特に鉱工業生産指数については、7月末に発生した熊本地震による影響が下押し要因になったとみら
れる。 大手自動車メーカーで工場の操業が停止した影響で自動車が前月比▲6.8％と大幅に減産したほ
か、 電子部品 ・ デバイスも同▲1.9％の減産となった。7月調査時点の生産予測指数は前月比＋3.1％（ 補
図表 2 各機関の実質GDP成長率予測値 図表 3 予測値の改定過程と各経済指標の寄与度

ト調査」等より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

質GDP成長率予測値。棒グラフは予測値に対する各経済指標の
寄与度を示す。「みずほGDPナウ」は各経済指標の結果を公表
前に予測しており （モデル予測値） 、 寄与度はモデル予測値と
実績値の乖離幅から計算される。 なお、 各経済指標の確報を受
けて指標が改定された場合も寄与度に反映される

### Page 3

3

正値ベース）と増産が見込まれていたが、大幅に下振れた格好だ。一方、世界的に旺盛なAI・データ
センター関連需要を背景に、半導体製造装置を含む生産用機械などが増産したものの、熊本地震の影
響を打ち返せなかった。消費財出荷指数の低下については、4～5月の耐久財特需が一巡したことで反
動減の動きが生じた。非耐久財の出荷は前月から増加したものの、7月の落ち込みも踏まえると、 基調
としては力強さを欠いていると言えよう。長雨や低温、といった天候不順により生鮮野菜価格が上昇
しているほか、食肉も値上がりが大きく、こうした手に取る機会が多い品目の価格上昇が家計の節約
志向を強め、個人消費の下押し圧力となっているとみられる。9月についても、食品のほか、原油・石
油化学製品等のコスト高の影響で幅広い品目の価格上昇が加速する見込みであり、個人消費は冴えな
い動きが続く可能性がある。 
以上のように、7～9月期成長率は低い伸びに抑えられる公算が大きい。前述のとおり原油・ナフサ
の輸入増、 企業の在庫調整といった動きはGDP統計上、成長率に対してマイナスに寄与する。加えて、
熊本地震による生産減や天候要因による消費支出抑制の動きが下押し要因になる見込みだ。 
次回の 「みずほGDPナウ」については、11月16日に公表される7～9月期GDP1次速報を挟んで、10～
12月期GDPのリアルタイム予測に取り組む。10月分の鉱工業生産や消費活動指数の結果等を踏まえて
推計し、12月中旬頃のレポート発刊を予定している。原油高等を受けた物価上昇圧力の強まりや堅調
なAI・データセンター関連需要など、プラス・マイナス双方の影響が交錯する中、ナウキャストによ
る景気動向の把握はより重要なものとなるだろう。

［参考文献］

けて～」 、内閣府経済社会総合研究所「経済分析」第208号

～DFMを用いた日本のGDPナウキャスティング～」 、 みずほリサーチ＆テクノロジーズ 『みずほ

1 ステップワイズ法により使用データを採択した。詳細は太田ほか（2024）を参照されたい。今後のモデルの予測精度のパフォ
ーマンス評価等を踏まえ、採択するデータについては見直しを行う可能性がある。 
2 詳細については、服部直樹「年率＋1.4％と 1 次速報から小幅に上方修正（4～6 月期2 次QE）」（2026 年9 月8 日）を参照され
たい。 
3 太田他（2024）で示した枠組みと同様、図表 2 の折れ線が各時点における実質GDP 成長率の予測値であり、月次の経済指標が
新たに公表されたり改定されたりすることで予測値が更新される。棒グラフは、予測値の改定幅、すなわち前回予測との差を各
月次経済指標で寄与度分解したもので、寄与度を合計するとモデル予測値の改定幅と一致する。なお、 「みずほGDP ナウ」は各
月次経済指標についても公表前に予測値を計算しており、各指標の寄与度はモデル予測値と実績値の乖離から計算されることか
ら、寄与度の符号は経済指標の変化の方向とは必ずしも一致しない。

労働分配率は低下傾向。賃金
以外の所得が家計所得を補完
米国：企業収益が拡大する一方、労働分配率は低下。金融所得や移転所得が消費を下支え
 企業収益が好調を維持する一方、労働所得の対GDP比は低下傾向。金融所得や政府からの移転所得が家計所得を補完
― 株式配当や社会保障給付など、賃金以外の所得が家計の購買力を支える構図

― 燃料高にもかかわらず、空港利用者数は前年並みの水準を維持し、小売売上高も高めの伸びを維持
― 年末商戦も前年を上回る伸びが予想されている

日本：コアCPIは10月に鈍化も、政策要因を除けばインフレ率は上昇基調
4
 10月の都区部コアＣＰＩは前年比＋2.6％（前月：同＋2.7％）と伸び鈍化
― 無償化期間の時期ズレによる9月水道料金の一時的な押し上げが剥落したことに加え、コメ価格の下落が影響
 ただし、政策要因を除けばインフレ率は上昇基調。サービス物価が底堅く推移する中、財物価の加速がインフレ率押し上げ
― 原油高等によるコスト増の転嫁が進展し、エネルギー、その他財（除く水道料金）の価格上昇が足元で加速
― 春先に鈍化した一般サービス物価（除く家賃）も人件費等の転嫁進展を映じて前年比＋2％近傍で推移

―10月は外食を中心に価格改定の動きがみられるも、広がりは限定的。一方、外食除く改定品目では値上げ幅が拡大
（注） 食品は、コメと外食を除く食料価格。水道料金はその他財に該当。サービスは一般

日本：企業の価格転嫁は進展も、消費マインドの弱さが追加値上げの重荷に
5
 企業の価格転嫁は着実に進展
― 日商LOBO調査では、コスト増加分の4割以上を価格転嫁できた企業が55.4％と4月から2.6％ポイント増加。ただし、労
務費の転嫁は40.6％にとどまり、販売単価DIも小幅低下するなど、中小企業では追加値上げの勢いに一服感
 消費者態度指数は2カ月連続で悪化。消費マインドの弱さは残存
― 物価高による節約志向が企業の追加値上げを制約している可能性。日銀は基調的な物価上昇率が2％に到達したと判
断し、上振れリスクへの警戒を強化。ただし、サービス価格の改定に広がりはみられず
（出所） 日本商工会議所「LOBO調査」より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

米国：10月FOMC～据え置き、ウォーシュ議長は引き締めがAIセクターにも影響との認識示す
6
 3.75～4.00％に据え置き（2人が反対、利上げを支持）。景気評価を上方修正しつつ、9月利上げ効果を見極め
― 景気評価は「堅調」から「力強い」へ。ウォーシュ議長は投資の広がりに言及
 AIセクターでは投資の選別が起きており、高い成長期待を持つ同セクターに対しても金融政策は有効との認識示す
 2027・2028年の会合の一部を政策決定を行わず、声明文も公表しない戦略的議論の会合とすると表明
AIセクター、生産性、タスクフォース声明文の変更、政策判断、会合運営
 反対2票は利上げを主張
 景気評価は「堅調」から「力強い」へ
 様子見による「情報のさらなる蓄積」
が、物価安定への「適時の復帰」のため
の政策判断に役立つ
声明文
 「以前より引き締まった」が「引き締め
的な水準にあるわけではない」と述べ、
追加利上げの余地残す
 「参加者の多数派は利上げ効果が出てい
ると認識」と言及

金融環境
と効果
 「政治は、この決定に何の役割も果たし
ていない」と言明 中間選挙
 2027年秋の1回、2028年には2回の会合
を政策決定せず議論だけの会合へ
 戦略的な熟議のための時間と余地確保
会合運営
 「投資家が期待する収益率はあまりに違
うので、AIには何の影響も及ばない」と
いう見方を否定
 「新技術と今回の投資急増」に対して金
融政策は有効
 信用スプレッドの拡大などは「市場参加
者が、どのような投資を行いたいか、資
本配分を選別している」ことの表れ
AI投資

上向いて」おり、高い経済成⾧率によっ
てインフレ圧力が生まれ、利上げが必要
な状況ではないとの認識

内容ポイント
 26年3Qの景気は引き続き拡大
 エネルギー価格と資金調達コストの上昇が今後の重しに景気
 エネルギー価格の見通しは9月の予想を上回っている
 今後数四半期の物価は目標を大幅に上回る見通し。その後
はエネルギー価格低下と金利上昇により目標水準に回帰へ
 物価の基調・賃金はおおむね安定。ただし、価格形成の初
期段階では上昇圧力がみられる
 今後6週間で得られる、基調的なインフレに関する多くの情
報を、とりわけ注視していく
物価
 9月理事会以降、金融環境は引き締まっている。金利は上昇
し、株価は下落している
 期待インフレの上昇やハイパースケーラーの社債発行等、
複合的な要因が金利を押し上げ。加えて、債務の多い加盟
国は固有の課題に直面
 市場の不当かつ無秩序な動向が金融政策伝達の妨げとな
る場合、対応手段は用意されている

 今後の金融政策はデータに基づき、会合ごとに決定

欧州：ECBは政策金利を据え置き。高金利は企業の資金需要を押し下げ
7
 ECBは預金ファシリティ金利を2.5％で据え置き。利上げが議論されるも物価の基調に著変なく、慎重な対応が適切と判断
― 利上げの要否検討にあたり、エネルギー価格の上昇と金融環境の引き締まりが主な論点に。今後は物価の基調に関す
る指標を特に注視する姿勢。ラガルド総裁は、進むべき方向が「きわめて明確」であると発言
― ラガルド総裁は2027年2月に早期退任へ。同年5月までに専務理事2名を含む計3名の幹部が交代する見込み
 10月の銀行貸出調査によれば、金利水準が企業の資金需要減少に寄与。高金利の影響は既に一部で顕在化
― 同調査によれば、企業向け貸出基準はフランスで一段と厳格化。銀行のリスク許容度低下やリスク認識の高まりが主因

企業向け銀行貸出の需要増減要因
（注） 需要増減は、企業向け貸出の需要が前四半期から「かなり増加」「やや増加」と回
答した銀行の割合から、「かなり減少」「やや減少」と回答した銀行の割合を引いた
もの。「その他要因」には他の調達手段による代替や、「運転資金・在庫」「設備投
資」以外の資金需要発生等を含む。2026年Q3の調査期間は9月21日～10月6日
（出所）ECBより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
10月ECB理事会のポイント（声明文・総裁会見）
（出所）ECBより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

### Page 9

英国：政府は財政規律を重視しつつエネルギー高対策に注力。財政余力は縮小
 英政府は秋季財政計画を公表。財政規律を重視しつつ家計や小規模事業者向け支援等を打ち出す内容
― 燃料税減税と電気料金へのVAT減税の延長、給付金支給によるエネルギー高対策、住宅購入支援などの家計支援策
を発表。小規模事業者の税負担軽減や電気料金の引き下げなど、企業向け支援も公表
― 上記政策の財源は銀行への追加課税やギャンブルへの課税強化等で確保する方針
― 防衛費は2030年までにGDP比3％へ引き上げる方針。ただし、財源を含む詳細は来年の歳出見直しに先送り
 財政余力は前回3月の237億ポンドから147億ポンドに大幅縮小。中東情勢悪化に伴う金利上昇等が背景
英国：財政余力（左）と3月以降の財政余力減少要因（右）
（出所）OBRより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
英国：秋季財政計画の主な内容
（出所） 英国政府より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
• 燃料税5ペンス減税や電気料金のVA T免除を来年7月まで実施
• 2,000 万世帯を対象に100ポンドのエネルギー高支援給付金支給

• 使用量にかかわらず課される家庭用ガスのスタンディングチャー
ジ廃止
• エネルギー集約型製造業1万社の電気代を20～25％軽減
• 手ごろな住宅建設に15億ポンド追加拠出。住宅初回購入者向け
支援「Your First Home」に47億ポンドを充当
• 小規模事業者の事業用不動産税免除対象を拡大。パブや音楽会
場等には同税を追加で20％軽減
• 食品等100件以上の品目の関税撤廃
• 防衛投資計画で必要とされていた150億ポンドの追加投資全額を
拠出。2030年に防衛費GDP比3％達成を企図。来年の歳出見直
しで財源の詳細公表
• 地方分権の一環として、来年から事業用不動産税のうち20％を地
方に移転、2030年までに100％への引き上げを目指すほか、所得
税も2028年から一部を地方に配分しインフラ整備の財源に
• 銀行への上乗せ課税を3％から6％に引き上げ
• 高額賭博機へのギャンブル税を20％から25％に引き上げ
• 貯蓄・不動産所得の税率を2％Pt引き上げ

英国：財政懸念を背景とする金利上昇リスクはくすぶり続ける見通し
 財政計画がおおむね事前の予想どおりの内容であったことなどから英10年債利回りの動きは限定的。ただし、足元の高金
利の要因であるインフレや財政懸念、世界的なAI関連投資増加は今後も続くとみられ、金利は高止まりが予想される

 利払い費による財政圧迫継続が予想される中、財政懸念を背景とする一段の金利上昇リスクはくすぶり続ける見通し
― 高金利継続による利払い負担が財政を圧迫。財政を巡る不確実性が高い状況は続く見通し
― 欧州各国では財政懸念や政情不安を背景に高債務国中心に国債利回り上昇
― 財政への懸念を払拭しにくい状況が続くなか、英国でも金利急騰とこれに伴う財政悪化リスクには要注意
政府債務残高と10年債利回り変化幅の関係

― 他方、鉄鋼は単月赤字に転落したほか、自動車も同▲68.5％の減益。過剰生産業種は厳しい状況
 7～9月期の製造業の売上高利益率は5.0％と4～6月期（5.5％）から低下。自動車など幅広い業種で前期を下回る
― コンピューター・通信機器は7.4％で、前年（4.3％）から急速に改善。資源高で非鉄金属加工も好調

― 他方、自動車は2.5％と、前年（3.8％）から大幅に悪化。過当競争による販売価格の低下が響いた格好

アジア：韓国・台湾では供給能力の拡大に向け、半導体産業の設備投資が活発化

― 韓国、台湾の主要な半導体・電子部品企業は、生産能力増強のため大規模な設備増強投資を計画
― 足元では韓国、台湾ともに半導体産業の機械設備投資が拡大傾向。主要各社による投資計画の進捗を示唆

 中東情勢緊迫化後、食料や燃料を中心にインフレ率が急上昇したタイとフィリピンでは、4～6月期に消費の伸びが減速

― タイでは燃料を中心とする交通費、フィリピンでは交通関連に加え、宿泊飲食なども抑制する形で消費者が節約行動
 背景に実質賃金の鈍化による購買力の低下
― 購買力低下を受け、タイは6月～11月に消費支援策（食品・日用品などの購入補助）を実施、フィリピンは政府による企
業への補助も伴いながら首都圏の最低賃金の大幅な引き上げを6月に発令。物価上昇への対応が両国の政治課題に

日本：基調的物価は2％到達も安定化には距離。日銀は利上げの影響を見極め微妙な舵取りへ
13
日銀10月会合のポイント（展望レポート・総裁会見）
（出所）日本銀行「経済・物価情勢の展望」より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
 日銀は基調的物価について「2％目標と概ね整合的な水準」に評価変更も、10月会合では利上げを見送り
― 今後は物価を2％で安定化させる局面へ。金融環境は緩和的との評価を維持し、次回12月会合で追加利上げの公算大
― 経済・物価はオントラックとの評価。原油価格の想定上振れを一部反映も見通し修正は軽微
― 総裁会見では「直近で物価上振れリスクが高まったわけではない」との発言。一段の利上げ加速は喫緊の必要性なし
 利上げを通じた基調的物価への影響を見極める手がかりとして、日銀は金融環境を注視

― 銀行・企業向けアンケート調査や銀行貸出残高にみられる変化、グローバル金融市場からの影響波及には要注意
経済・物価見通し
（注） 青字＝上昇修正、赤字＝下方修正。ドバイ原油価格は1バレル105ドル程度を出発
点に70ドル程度まで下落していくと前提。飲食料品消費税率の引き下げの影響は
織り込まず
（出所） 日本銀行より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
全体として修正は軽微。日銀見通し
対比でオントラックとの評価
展望レポート
• 基調的物価上昇率は2%物価目標と概ね整合的な水準
• 実質金利は上昇しているが、短期中心になお低水準。資金需要、
資産市場動向等も踏まえると金融環境は依然緩和した状態
• 名目賃金は前年並みのベースアップが実現するもとで、着実な増
加が続く見込み
• 海外の多くの中央銀行が利上げ方向に政策を転換。グローバルな
金融環境の変化がわが国の経済・物価に及ぼす影響に留意
会見中の総裁発言
• 物価上振れリスクはなおも無視できないが、1カ月前から物価上振
れリスクは大きく高まったわけではない

• 基調的物価が2%にアンカーされている度合いは極めて低い
• 金融政策が適時適切に発動され、緩和度合いを調整していくこと
で期待が安定化されていく
• 本支店ヒアリングでは、中小零細を中心に資金繰りがタイトになり
つつあるという声が聞かれ、今後の広がりを注意深くみていく
• 早めの政策対応を基本方針とするべき状況までは至っていない

日本：AI関連財の増産を背景に設備投資は今後拡大も、輸入依存の高さが成長の抑制要因に
14
 9月鉱工業生産は前月比＋2.2％と3カ月ぶりに増産。26年に入ってから増産基調を維持
― 増産の主因はAI関連。旺盛なAI関連需要を背景に、半導体製造装置などの増産が鉱工業生産をけん引
― 加えて、自動車生産の持ち直しなど、熊本地震や豪雨による下振れからの回復も生産の押し上げに寄与
 AI関連を中心に増産基調の生産動向は、今後の設備投資拡大を示唆
― ただし、資本財の輸入依存度は上昇傾向。AI関連業種で設備不足感が強まるなか、資本財の供給を輸入に頼る構造
― 人手不足などの供給制約で国内メーカーの増産が追いつかず、拡大する投資需要の一部が海外に流出の可能性も

（注）AI関連はWTO によるAI-enabling productsの定義に準ずる。3カ月後方移動平均

## Document 067: 2026年10月贸易数据点评：外贸增长的价格权重持续上升

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年10月贸易数据点评：外贸增长的价格权重持续上升.pdf
Report source: 西南证券
Date: 2026-11-11
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

请务必阅读正文后的重要声明部分  
1 
ooo[Table_ReportInfo] 2026 年 11 月 10 日      
证 券研究报告 •宏观简评报告      
数据点评  
外贸增长的价格权重 持续 上升 
——2026 年 10 月贸易数据点评

点评

西 南证券研究院  
[Table_Summary]  摘要：从贸易总体情况看，以美元计，2026 年 1—10 月我国货物贸易进出口
总额同比增长 25.3%，较 1—9 月扩大 1.5 个百分点。10 月单月出口同比增长
35.2%， 进口同比增长41.6%， 分别较上月回落1.1个百分点、 加快10.6 个百
分点， 进口增速创2021年 6 月以来新高， 贸易顺差收窄至1052.8亿美元。 展
望后续，去年同期低基数及主要经济体制造业PMI 仍处扩张区间，有望支撑
11 月外贸增速维持较高水平， 但前期集中出货效应减弱、 海外需求波动及贸易
政策不确定性或带来扰动。从贸易伙伴看，10 月我国对主要贸易伙伴出口增
速普遍回落，但对韩国出口同比增长92.4%，较上月加快 24.3 个百分点；自
韩国进口同比增长 110.2%，较上月加快15.9个百分点。对韩贸易高增主要受
AI 产业链涨价及加工贸易重复计算效应共同推动，若后续AI 硬件价格见顶回
落，对韩名义贸易增速或随之放缓。从出口结构看，机电产品、高新技术产品
出口金额同比分别增长46.1%、66.6%，自动数据处理设备及其零部件、集成
电路出口分别增长 114.8%、137.1%，AI 算力基础设施建设持续提供需求支撑。
不过，集成电路出口数量同比下降 2.5%，金额高增与实际出口数量增长存在
明显背离。汽车及零配件出口金额分别增长37.6%、32.1%，通用机械设备出
口增长 34.5%，出口动能有所扩散；手机、家电等传统消费电子出口增速边际
放缓，劳动密集型产品出口表现相对偏弱。从进口结构看，机电产品、高新技
术产品进口金额同比涨幅分别扩大至57.5%、72.1%，集成电路、自动数据处
理设备及其零部件进口分别增长 78.3%、261.0%，但集成电路进口数量同比
持平，反映 AI 产业链进口增长尚未同步体现为实物数量扩张。资源品方面，
原油、 成品油进口金额分别增长41.0%、45.2%， 但进口数量仍分别下降13.3%、
10.2%；铁矿石进口金额同比下降11.5%，铜及铜材进口金额增长45.6%，资
源品进口表现分化。汽车进口金额和数量分别下降38.5%、43.1%，农产品及
粮食、大豆进口也有所收缩。整体来看，10 月外贸增长仍主要由 AI 产业链及
部分资源品价格上涨支撑，出口结构向电子产业链集中，进口则呈现高技术产
品偏强、传统资源品及汽车偏弱的分化特征，剔除价格因素后，实际贸易需求
仍有待进一步改善。 
 进口超预期提速，顺差收窄。按美元计，2026年 1-10 月，我国货物贸易进出
口总额同比增长 25.3%，涨幅相比 1‑ 9 月扩大 1.5 个百分点，持续保持高位。
10 月单月，货物贸易出口同比增长 35.2%，较上月回落 1.1 个百分点，高于
wind 一致预测值（33.25%）；进口同比增长41.6%，较上月回升 10.6 个百分
点，显著高于 wind 一致预测值（30.36%），创 2021年 6 月以来最快同比增
速。贸易顺差为 1052.8 亿美元， 虽高于去年同期， 低于wind 一致预测值 （1195
亿美元），为 5 月以来最小单月顺差。关税与贸易谈判的不确定性催生企业前
置出货， 是今年出口高增的重要推手之一；不过随着窗口期临近，前期订单已
逐渐兑现，该支撑的边际贡献逐步回落。 同时，近期人民币升值放缓对美元计
价出口的折算贡献有所回落。进口端的高增则主要来自 AI 产业链产品与资源
品涨价，叠加去年同期低基数的驱动。从海外国际景气表现来看，10 月美欧
日制造业景气均有所回落：标普全球美国制造业 PMI 下修至 54.9，新订单增
速降至三月以来最低水平，反映出关税对国际销售的影响。能源、燃料、运输

温与内需疲弱交织  (2026-10-23)

2026 年 9 月经济数据点评  
(2026-10-20) 
7. 国内确立健康优先战略地位， 美国通胀
反弹推升加息预期  (2026-10-16) 
8. 政府债融资支撑增强， 政策成效静待释
放——2026 年 9 月社融数据点评  
(2026-10-16)

### Page 2

请务必阅读正文后的重要声明部分
2
和金属价格上涨叠加关税影响， 推动投入成本通胀升至五月以来最高水平；欧
元区制造业 PMI 终值回落至 52.7，产出增速放缓至四个月低点，但整体仍保
持稳健；日本制造业 PMI 终值回落至 53.3，企业产出和新订单增幅均放缓，
且增长速度都降至七个月来最低。贸易政策方面，11 月 9 日国务院关税税则
委员会发布公告，宣布自 11 月 10 日起 2 个月内继续暂停 24%的对美加征关
税、保留 10%税率。此次展期安排有助于稳定外贸企业对美订单与产能预期，
为后续谈判保留政策缓冲期。近期美方同步将 178 项对华 301 关税豁免延期至
2027 年 1 月 9 日，豁免清单以工业中间品、医疗用品、光伏制造设备为主，
有助于降低相关品类对美出口关税成本。往后看，去年四季度基数较低， 主要
经济体制造业 PMI也仍在扩张区间，11月贸易增速仍有望维持在较高水平，
但动能或边际放缓。出口方面，存储芯片价格上涨仍将提供支撑。进口方面，
芯片需求有望继续支撑年底进口增速， 而原油价格环比走弱可能拖累资源品进

口金额。整体来看，AI 硬件仍是影响进出口表现的核心变量，但剔除价格因
素后，真实外需与内需信号或仍相对偏弱。
 单月对韩贸易实现高增，低基数叠加 AI 链涨价共同支撑。以美元计，从出口
总额看，10 月单月我国对主要贸易伙伴出口同比增速普遍边际回落，但是对
韩国出口同比增速边际加快。对美国、东盟出口同比增速分别回落 6.6、5.9
个百分点至 32.9%、46.3%。对韩出口增速加快 24.3 个百分点至 92.4%。从
进口总额看，除欧盟以外，10 月单月我国对主要贸易伙伴的进口同比增速均
加快。对韩国、美国、日本、东盟进口同比增速分别加快 15.9、14.2、11.1、
5.8 个百分点至 110.2%、35.0%、26.5%、42.0%。从韩国前 20 日贸易数据看，
10 月 1-20 日，韩国半导体出口额同比增长222.5%，占出口总额的 44.7%。
本月我国自韩国进口金额高增主要受 AI 产业链产品价格上涨推动，对韩出口
高增则来自加工贸易的重复计算效应。同一批芯片的中间形态在双边贸易统计

中可能被计入了不止一次， 而芯片价格的上涨会同时放大进口额和出口额。 若
后续 AI 硬件价格见顶回落，对韩名义出口增速可能随之回落，且实际贸易量
未必出现同等幅度的收缩。此外，10 月中欧就混动汽车贸易安排达成谅解，
拟设置首年 35万辆的进口配额，次年起配额每年上调6%，措施最早拟于 12
月 1 日生效。该配额机制名义适用于所有来源地混动进口， 因此对欧汽车出口
的边际拖累或在年底至明年初逐步显现。
 出口品类集中度提升，AI 产业链为景气主线。从出口金额看，以美元计，10
月单月机电产品、高新技术产品出口金额同比分别增长46.1%、66.6%，增速
较上月分别加快 2.4、3.0 个百分点， 占出口总额的比重分别为67.42%、33.56%
（两类统计口径存在交叉，部分产品同时归入两类），说明出口高增品类进一
步向电子链条集中。 分产品看，AI 相关电子产品出口表现突出，自动数据处理
设备及其零部件、集成电路出口金额同比分别增长114.8%、137.1%，增速较
上月分别加快 21.8、1.1个百分点。 传统消费电子出口增速则有所放缓， 手机、

家用电器、 音视频设备及其零件出口金额同比分别增长13.2%、20.9%、12.1%，
增速较上月均边际放缓。汽车包括底盘、汽车零配件出口金额同比分别增长
37.6%、32.1%，而包括箱包、服装、鞋靴等品类的传统劳动密集型产品同比
增速均边际回落，显示传统轻工外需承压。通用机械设备出口金额同比上升
34.5%，增速边际加快 19 个百分点，或反映海外制造业资本开支回暖，全球
中游设备需求边际改善。成品油出口金额同比涨幅收窄 44.8 个百分点至
80.3%。从出口数量看，重点电子产品呈现明显量价分化。集成电路出口数量
同比由上月上升 0.9%转为下降 2.5%， 说明本月集成电路出口金额的爆发几乎
完全由单价上涨贡献。 手机出口数量同比下降18.2%，降幅进一步扩大， 出口
金额增长主要受高端机型占比提升和产品单价上涨带动，而出口数量降幅扩
大，则反映全球手机终端需求仍显疲弱，也可能与部分低端组装订单持续向海
外转移有关。 汽车包括底盘、 家用电器出口数量同比分别上升33.5%、14.1%，

但涨幅均较上月收窄，或由于节前出货、赶关税窗口等因素释放后，增速自然
回落。其中，汽车品类出口量价齐升， 但汽车出口数量增速中枢已从二季度的

### Page 3

请务必阅读正文后的重要声明部分
3
极端水平回落， 或反映汽车出口增量逻辑从单纯放量逐步向结构升级、单价抬
升切换。往后看，年内出口结构或延续向电子产业链集中的特征，但需关注价
格上涨与实际出口数量的背离。 传统消费电子需求偏弱， 手机出口数量或继续
承压；汽车出口则受前期集中出货效应消退影响，增速可能进一步回落。
 涨价驱动 AI 链进口高增，资源品进口读数受价格支撑。从进口金额看，以美
元计， 机电产品、 高新技术产品进口金额同比涨幅分别扩大至57.5%、72.1%，
反映进口增长进一步向电子产业链集中。 分产品看，AI 相关电子产品进口保持
高增长，集成电路进口金额同比涨幅小幅扩大至78.3%，自动数据处理设备及
其零部件进口金额同比上升261.0%，涨幅较上月收窄35.5 个百分点。能源资
源品进口金额有所回升， 原油进口金额同比上升41.0%， 涨幅较上月扩大37.7
个百分点，成品油进口金额同比由上月下降转为上升45.2%。铁矿砂及其精矿
进口金额同比下降 11.5%，降幅较上月扩大， 说明黑色系原料的进口价值量有

所收缩； 铜矿砂及其精矿进口金额同比上升23.1%，涨幅较上月收窄，未锻造
的铜及铜材进口金额同比上升45.6%，涨幅较上月扩大。10 月 LME铜期货结
算均价同比上升 35.9%，涨幅较上月收窄约 10 个百分点，但价格同比仍维持
较高正增，或指向铜进口金额高增主要由铜价处于历史高位拉动。 汽车包括底
盘进口金额同比下降38.5%，降幅较上月扩大；农产品、粮食和大豆进口金额
同比均由上月上升转为下降。从进口数量看，电子产品进口呈现价格强、 数量
弱的特征。集成电路进口数量同比持平，说明AI 资本开支周期对价格周期与
订单节奏高度敏感，而实物数量的需求并未同步显著扩张。 能源品进口金额增
长主要受到价格推动。原油、成品油进口数量同比分别下降13.3%、10.2%，
降幅较上月分别收窄8.2、42.9 个百分点。10 月布伦特原油现货均价为125.0
美元/桶，同比上升 92.3%，涨幅较上月扩大近20 个百分点。在原油进口数量
同比仍在下降的背景下， 原油进口金额高增主要由价格弹性主导。 汽车及农产

品进口则延续数量收缩。汽车包括底盘进口数量同比下降43.1%，降幅较上月
收窄 6.9个百分点，本月汽车进口量价两端均明显收缩，而数量端收缩幅度更
大，反映国产车全面替代、进口车市场结构性退潮的长期趋势。粮食和大豆进
口数量同比降幅均有所扩大，本月农产品量价两端均为收缩，反映饲料需求偏
弱，粮食储备节奏偏慢。往后看，集成电路进口金额有望延续较高增长， 但进
口数量增长偏弱， 价格因素仍是重要驱动。资源品方面， 原油价格走弱或削弱
进口金额增速，铜价仍可能提供支撑。整体来看，进口增长仍集中于AI 相关
硬件，传统资源品及消费品进口需求尚未出现全面改善。
 风险提示：国内政策落地不及预期，海外经济波动超预期， 地缘政治风险超预
期。

分析师承诺  
本报告署名分析师具有中国证券业协会授予的证券投资咨询执业资格并注册为证券分析师，报告所采用的数据均
来自合法合规渠道，分析逻辑基于分析师的职业理解，通过合理判断得出结论，独立、客观地出具本报告。分析师承
诺不曾因，不因，也将不会因本报告中的具体推荐意见或观点而直接或间接获取任何形式的补偿。

投资评级说明  
报告中投资建议所涉及的评级分为公司评级和行业评级（另有说明的除外）。评级标准为报告发布日后 6 个月内
的相对市场表现，即：以报告发布日后 6 个月内公司股价（或行业指数）相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数的涨跌幅
作为基准。其中：A 股市场以沪深 300 指数为基准， 新三板市场以三板成指 （针对协议转让标的） 或三板做市指数 （针
对做市转让标的）为基准；香港市场以恒生指数为基准；美国市场以纳斯达克综合指数或标普 500 指数为基准。  
公司评级  
买入：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅在 20%以上  
持有：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅介于 10%与 20%之间 
中性：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅介于 -10%与 10%之间  
回避：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅介于 -20%与-10%之间  
卖出：未来 6 个月内，个股相对同期相关证券市场代表性指数涨幅在 -20%以下  
行业评级  
强于大市：未来 6 个月内，行业整体回报高于同期相关证券市场代表性指数 5%以上 
跟随大市：未来 6 个月内，行业整体回报介于同期相关证券市场代表性指数 -5%与 5%之间 
弱于大市：未来 6 个月内，行业整体回报低于同期相关证券市场代表性指数 -5%以下

Bull/Bear: Inventec (2356.TW)

### Page 6

Inventec (2356.TW)
10 November 2026 Citi Research
6
Inventec
Company description
Established in 1975 and listed in 1996, Taiwan-based Inventec is a key ODM
of NBs, servers, smartphones, and wearable/Io T devices to global customers.
Major shareholders include the founder Mr. Yeh Kuo-I, whose family has (both
directly and indirectly) a c35% stake.
Investment strategy
We rate Inventec shares as Buy. We expect stable demand in NB and server to
help support near-term earnings, while product diversification within smart

devices to areas such as Io T and medical space should help improve L-T
margins. We expect the company's solid cash yield to serve as a downside
cushion.
Valuation
Our target price of NT$65 for Inventec shares is based on 17x 2027 EPS, which
is slightly above the mid-point of its historical trading range, reflecting the
company's product diversification and stable demand outlook led by servers
and commercial NBs, as well as increasing AI server exposure that could

potentially drive margin expansion and better earnings momentum.
Risks
Key downside risks that could impede the stock from reaching our target price
include: 1) Slower-than-expected shipment growth/weaker-than-expected
sell-through in HomePods and AirPods could lead to downside in our sales
forecasts; 2) If competitors adopt aggressive pricing strategies to gain market
shares, our ASP and margin assumptions could face downside risk; 3) Lower-
than-expected server margins driven by rising exposure to Chinese customers

could impact our earnings forecasts negatively; 4) Deterioration in NB
demand and investment in solar would hurt our forecasts; and 5) Stronger-
than-expected Taiwan dollar could lead to FX loss and downside to our
current forecasts.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

M
Update
Quanta Computer Inc.  | Asia Pacific
3Q26 OpM miss; stay OW
Management said AI demand has remained unchanged in recent
weeks. It reiterates confidence in strong double-digit y/y growth
into 2027. General server demand continues to exceed
expectations YTD, driven by agentic AI.
Platform transition weighs on 4Q shipments; VR200 to support CY27 growth: AI
servers contributed 75–80% of 3Q26 server revenue, with CY26 AI revenue on track
for triple-digit y/y growth and ~65% of Quanta’s total revenue. The platform

transition is expected to drive a q/q decline in 4Q26 NVL72 rack shipments, but
management’s tone suggests a milder decline than our prior forecast of 4K racks (vs.
~6.2K in 3Q26). We therefore raise our 4Q26 NVL72 production forecast 1K, to 5K,
implying 21.9K builds in CY26. Management expects shipments to accelerate in 1Q27
as VR200 ramps, supporting high double-digit AI server revenue growth in CY27.
ASIC servers’ revenue contribution is projected to double, from high single digits in

CY26 to double digits in CY27.
Agentic AI supports general server growth: Management noted that YTD general
server shipments have exceeded expectations, with both shipments and revenue
increasing q/q in 3Q26 and further growth expected in 4Q26, primarily driven by
agentic AI. Management expects steady double-digit y/y growth in both shipments
and revenue in CY27, with deployments broadly balanced between x86 and Arm-
based platforms and comparable margin profiles. While industry-wide component

shortages persist, particularly in memory, CPUs and PCBs, management indicated
that tier-1 CSPs continue to secure sufficient supply.
Lower NB volumes and rising costs pressure margins: Quanta’s NB shipments fell
17% q/q, to 9.6M units in 3Q26, weaker than normal seasonality but above guidance
of a 20% decline. Rising memory costs and lower scale weighed on profitability, and
we expect margin pressure to persist in 4Q26. Management guides for another

double-digit q/q shipment decline in 4Q26, albeit narrower than in 3Q26, implying a
15–20% y/y decline for CY26. We project 4Q26 shipments of 8.3M units (-14% q/q)
and CY26 shipments of 39.4M units (-15% y/y). For CY27, management expects NB
shipments to decline in high single digits to 10% y/y and PC/NB revenue to fall to a
single-digit share of the mix, reflecting AI server racks’ substantially higher ASPs.
Keep price target at NT$385 and stay OW: Our target implies 11.8x CY27e P/E or

11.0x CY28e P/E, vs. its three-year range of 6-18x. Risk-reward looks attractive at  9.1x
CY27e P/E, below the three-year average of 11.7x, with ~5.1% dividend yield (DPS of
NT$15.6).

### Page 4

M
Update
4
3Q26 Results Summary
Component cost pressure drove 3Q GM miss:
Revenue was NT$1,115.5bn (+8% q/q, +125% y/y), but GM deteriorated to 4.8% (-30bps q/
q, -210bps y/y), which is 40bps below our estimate and 20bps below the Street.
Management cited the  following reasons for the GM miss:
• PC/NB: cost pressures from inflationary component pricing; diminishing economies
of scale.
• Servers: inflationary component pricing pressured server gross margins, despite a

slight decline in AI server shipments.
However, opex was also higher than expected, mainly owing to higher R&D expenses.
Thus, OP came in at NT$32.2bn (-6% q/q, +75% y/y), 7%  below our estimate and 112%
below the Street.
OpM was 2.9% (-40bps q/q, -80bps y/y). Management guided for 2H OpM to be flat h/h,
so we think 3Q OpM was a miss.
Non-op income was higher than expected because of ~NT$3.9bn in FX gains, so net profit
came in at ~NT$28.4bn (-1% q/q, +73% y/y) for EPS of NT$7.36, 6% above our estimate

and 2% above the Street.
Consignment? Under the buy-and-sell model, high-ASP server rack procurement inflated
revenue but diluted gross margins and to a lesser extent operating margins (owing to
operating leverage) via pass-through component accounting. Management continues to
stress absolute profit expansion and sustained >20% ROIC over margin ratios. To mitigate
balance sheet inventory carrying risk and working capital burden, Quanta will continue to

negotiate for a consignment business model with its CSP customers into CY27.

lowering our margin assumptions for 2026-2028.

### Page 6

M
Update
6
Price Target and Valuation Methodology
We keep our price target at NT$385, reflecting the offset between raised 2026-28
earnings estimates and a lower medium-term growth rate: This implies 11.8x CY27e and
11.0x CY28e EPS, in line with the average of Quanta's three-year historical range of 11.7x.
We view this as reasonable because we expect the company to benefit from rising AI
server and EV demand over the long term, which will offset the weakness in the PC

segment and drive mix improvements.
We continue to apply a residual income model to derive our price target. Our key
assumptions include:
• A cost of equity of 9.0% (beta of 1.2, equity premium of 6.0%, and risk-free rate of
1.5%)
• A 7.0% medium-term growth rate – lowered from 8.5% previously, as we bake in
some conservatism on concerns over a potential slowdown in data center build-
out following the US midterm elections (see our Global Team's view), which could

affect Quanta's server business directly.
• A 3% terminal growth rate
Our bull and bear case scenario values also stay unchanged at $545 and NT$230,
respectively.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

## Document 225: [005380] 현대차(005380) 어려운 업황 속 돋보이는 방어력현대차(005380) 어려운 업황 속 돋보이는 방어력어려운 업황 속 돋보이는 방어력 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[005380] 현대차(005380) 어려운 업황 속 돋보이는 방어력현대차(005380) 어려운 업황 속 돋보이는 방어력어려운 업황 속 돋보이는 방어력 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-10-30
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

매수 (유지)  
26년 3분기, 물량 감소에도 확인된 이익 방어력 
현대차의 3Q26 매출액은 45조 1,100억원(Y oY -3.4%), 영업이익은 2조 6,770억원
(Y oY +5.5%), OPM 5.9%(Y oY +0.5%p)를 기록했다. 국내 파업 및 영업일수 감소 영
향으로 글로벌 도매 판매량은 92.1만대(Y oY -11.3%)까지 감소했으나, HEV 중심의 
고부가 차종 Mix 개선, 기말환율 하락에 따른 판매보증비(Y oY -1.2조원) 감소 효과
로 수익성은 오히려 개선됐다. 특히 HEV 판매 비중은 18.2%(Y oY +2.7%p)까지 상
승했다. 반면 EV 판매는 전년 대비 감소세가 이어지며 지역별 수요 둔화와 경쟁 심
화 영향이 지속됐다. 
연간 눈높이는 낮아졌지만, 믹스 개선은 지속 
현대차는 글로벌 수요 둔화와 생산 차질을 반영해 2026년 글로벌 도매 판매 목표를 
기존 415.8만대에서 400만대로, 영업이익률 가이던스를 기존 6.3~7.3%에서 
5.5~6.5%로 하향 조정했다. 다만 북미를 중심으로 HEV 판매가 견조하게 유지되고 
있으며, 하반기 주요 신차 투입과 전동화 라인업 확대를 통해 믹스 개선 기조는 이
어질 전망이다. 중장기적으로는 로보택시 공급 확대와 Atlas 상용화 등 Physical AI 
사업도 계획대로 진행 중으로, 단기 판매 성장 둔화에도 자동차 본업의 수익성 방어
와 신사업 모멘텀은 유효한 것으로 판단한다. 
투자의견 매수 유지, 목표주가 460,000원 하향 
글로벌 완성차 업체들의 수익성 부담은 확대되고 있다. 3분기 자동차 부문 영업이익
률은 Mercedes-Benz 2.5%, Stellantis 0.7%, Volvo -5.6%, VW -7.8%를 기록했다. 
유럽 내 BEV 판매 확대에도 중국 전기차 업체들의 공격적인 해외 진출과 가격 경
쟁, 중국 시장 부진이 동시에 나타나면서 전통 OEM의 수익성 압박이 심화되고 있
다. 이러한 환경에서 현대차는 HEV 중심의 유연한 파워트레인 전략과 북미 중심의 
Mix 개선을 통해 5% 후반의 수익성을 유지하고 있다는 점이 상대적으로 부각된다.

Compliance Notice 
동 자료에 게재된 내용들은 외부의 압력이나 부당한 간섭없이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하여 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
동 자료는 기관투자가 또는 제3자에게 사전 제공한 사실이 없습니다. 
동 자료는 조사분석자료 작성에 참여한 외부인(계열회사 및 그 임직원등)이 없습니다. 
조사분석 담당자 및 배우자는 해당종목과 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 
동 자료에 언급된 종목의 지분율 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
당사는 상기 명시한 사항 외 고지해야 하는 특별한 이해관계가 없습니다.

종목명 담당자 담당자(배우자) 보유여부 1%이상 보유여부 유가증권 발행관련 계열사 관계여부 공개매수 사무취급 IPO 회사채 지급보증 중대한 이해관계 M&A 관련 수량 취득가 취득일

## Document 321: Consumer Panel update Q3 2026_ More resilience (2026-11-13)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Consumer Panel update Q3 2026_ More resilience (2026-11-13).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-13
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Past performance is not a reliable indicator of future performance. The forecasts given above are predictive in character. Whilst every effort has been taken to ensure that the assumptions on which the forecasts are based are reasonable, 
the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
1
WESTPAC ECONOMICS  
CONSUMER PANEL Q3 2026 
MORE RESILIENCE
3 November 2026
• Average consumer spend across 
the Westpac-DataX Consumer 
Panel remained resilient, rising 
1.5% in Q3 and 4.6%yr. 
• While some of this reflected a price-
driven surge in fuel spend (+0.3%qtr) 
discretionary categories also posted 
robust gains (contributing 0.9ppts 
to the headline rise) with non-fuel 
essential categories contributing 
positively as well (+0.5ptps).
• Households continued to add to savings 
and buffers. Savings rose 1.3%qtr, led 
by younger households and mortgagors, 
with average savings buffers increasing 
to 22.2 months of mortgage repayments. 
• The detail shows financial pressures 
remain uneven but spending patterns 
appear to be converging across 
consumers with low, medium and high 
buffers. Much of this appears to relate 
to a reduction in fuel consumption.

Westpac–DataX Consumer Panel
The Westpac–DataX Consumer Panel is a large 
dataset that gives a timely and detailed picture 
of Australian consumer finances and behaviour. 
Developed by DataX, Westpac’s data analytics 
team, the dataset links transaction activity with 
balance sheet information to give a complete view 
of income, spending, saving and borrowing flows. 
All data is de-identified and aggregated to 
ensure privacy. The resulting sample of over 
one million customers gives an accurate 
representation of trends across the wider 
Australian consumer and is perfectly suited 
to quickly tracing responses to events like 
interest rate moves and energy price shocks.
Powered by 
Fuel and non-fuel spending lift

WESTPAC ECONOMICS
Heatmap of monthly spending categories
The Westpac DataX Consumer Panel  shows average
consumer spending remained resilient in Q3, rising 1.5%
despite a further lift in fuel costs. Fuel contributed 0.3ppts
to quarterly growth, but discretionary spending did more

of the heavy lifting, contributing 0.9ppts as recreation
and travel-related spending recovered. Households also
continued to add to their financial buffers: savings up 1.3%
in the quarter and average buffers across households with
a mortgage increasing to 22.2 months of repayments.
Beneath the aggregate picture, lower-buffer households
remain more exposed to higher fuel costs and mortgage
repayments, but their discretionary spending share is
looking increasingly similar to other households.

Spending holds up despite higher fuel costs
Average household spending increased 1.5% in Q3, with much
of the strength again driven by transport-related spending.
Fuel contributed 0.3ppts to quarterly growth, while transport
excluding fuel contributed a further 0.4ppts. Excluding the
direct effects of the price-driven lift in fuel, average spend
was up 1.2%qtr. Importantly, stronger fuel spending did not
come at the expense of discretionary purchases. Discretionary

categories contributed 0.9ppts to quarterly spending growth,
compared with 0.5ppts from non-fuel essentials.
Solid rise in non-fuel spend and financial buffers
Discretionary spending dominates
Recreation and culture contributed 0.3ppts to growth, reflecting
stronger spending on sporting services, while restaurants and
hotels added a further 0.2ppts. Transport spending was also
boosted by a rebound in airline expenditure, which more than
unwound the weakness recorded in Q2 when refunds relating

to cancellations due to conflict in the Middle East weighed on
the category. Discretionary spending rose 0.9%qtr, following a
flat result and a 0.1% rise in the previous two quarters.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS  
Savings growth by mortgage status
Households continue to rebuild savings
Savings increased 1.3% in Q3, with the strongest gains again 
recorded among younger households. Savings balances for 
18–34 year olds increased 2.0%qtr, compared with 0.7% 
for those aged 55 and over. Mortgagors also continued to 
accumulate savings more rapidly, recording a 2.8% increase 
compared with 0.7% among non-mortgagors. Savings growth 
among mortgagors has now outpaced non-mortgagors for 
three consecutive years.
Stronger savings have continued to lift mortgage buffers. 
Average buffers (savings divided by the sum of essential 
expenses and minimum mortgage repayments), increased to 
22.2 months of repayments in September. Looking within the 
unit-record sample, median mortgage buffers jumped from 
6.3 months in August to 6.8 months, its highest level since the 
series began in 2019.

Median mortgage buffers
Fuel spending a clear drag on consumers
Fuel spending as a share of total spending
Spending outcomes continued to vary across age groups. 
The squeeze remains most apparent among younger 
households. Fuel spending rose 5.1%qtr for 18–34 year olds, 
against a 1.5%qtr increase in non-fuel spending. By contrast, 
non-fuel spending increased 2.1%qtr among households aged 
55 and over. 
The gap between mortgagors and non-mortgagors was 
more modest. Spending rose 1.6%qtr among mortgagors 
and 1.9%qtr among those without a mortgage, suggesting 
mortgage status alone masks considerable variation in 
household financial positions – a divide that becomes clearer 
when households are grouped by the size of their mortgage 
buffers (see discussion further on in this note). Note that ‘non 
mortgagors’ covers a diverse range of consumers including 
those that are renting and those that own their homes without 
a mortgage.

WESTPAC ECONOMICS  
Median litres of fuel consumed
More importantly, their consumption appears to be less 
responsive to price movements. During periods of cheaper 
fuel, high-buffer households show a noticeably larger lift in 
litres purchased, consistent with stockpiling when prices are 
favourable. Low-buffer households show much less of this 
response, while their fuel volumes have also adjusted less 
during the recent increase in fuel costs.
The spending data show the consequence. Fuel expenditure 
has risen sharply across all three groups recently, but remains 
highest among low-buffer households, where it also absorbs 
a larger share of total spending. Food spending has also 
declined since 2023. This may partly reflect households 
economising within the category, including switching towards 
cheaper or home-brand products, although further work is 
needed to test this.
Taken together, the households with the smallest savings 
cushions face higher mortgage repayments, spend more 
on fuel and appear to have less scope to adjust their fuel 
consumption when prices change. 
Discretionary spend as a share of total
This suggests that in aggregate, household balance sheets 
are looking healthy. The picture is a little at odds with the very 
weak reads on our Westpac-Melbourne Index Consumer 
Sentiment Index which suggests finances are coming under 
much more pressure. The fact that both average spending and 
saving both rose suggests incomes over the quarter were not 
overly stretched and that more of the weakness in sentiment 
may relate to concerns about the outlook (something that may 
also be encouraging consumers to build buffers now, while they 
still can).
Pressures intense for low-buffer households
Aggregate buffer measures mask sizeable differences across 
mortgagors. We can get a sense of these variations by 
combining mortgagors into three broad groups according to 
buffer size, i.e. low, middle and high buffer groups. This shows 
that households with the smallest buffers are also more 
exposed to both interest rates and fuel costs.

The buffer differences are relatively stark, the median savings 
of the low-buffer group just $2k compared to $15k for the 
middle-buffer group and $95k for the high buffer group. 
Low buffer households face median minimum mortgage 
repayments of around $2,600 a month, compared with $2,400 
for the middle buffer group and $2,200 for the highest-buffer 
group. 
Low buffer households also spend more on fuel, at around 
$170 a month compared with $160 for the middle buffer 
group and $150 for the high buffer group.

We can use average pump prices to estimate what this 
expenditure implies for fuel volumes. Low-buffer households 
consume around 70 litres a month, middle-buffer households 
67 litres a month and the high-buffer group around 62 litres. 
This compares to an average of 81, 80 and 70 respectively 
through 2024-25. 
Median fuel spend by customer

### Page 5

Past performance is not a reliable indicator of future performance. The forecasts given above are predictive in character. Whilst every effort has been taken to ensure that the assumptions on which the forecasts are based are reasonable, 
the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially from these forecasts.
5
WESTPAC ECONOMICS  
Yet despite this greater exposure, the gap in broader spending 
behaviour has narrowed. High-buffer households continue 
to spend more on discretionary categories, averaging around 
$1,940 a month compared with $1,740 for low-buffer 
households. However, their discretionary share has been 
broadly stable since 2023, while the shares of low- and 
middle-buffer households have risen by close to a percentage 
point bringing them more in line with the high-buffer group.

Some of the convergence reflects a longer run down-trend in 
fuel spending that has been more pronounced amongst low 
and middle buffer households. This lowering in fuel spend lifts 
the share available for other categories.

Essential spending excluding fuel, meanwhile, has changed 
little across the three cohorts since 2023. The shift in 
spending patterns has largely occurred on the discretionary 
side.

The result is a somewhat mixed picture. Low-buffer 
households remain considerably more exposed to mortgage 
and fuel costs and have far smaller savings cushions. But their 
discretionary spending has nevertheless moved closer to that 
of better-buffered households, suggesting some recovery in 
spending flexibility despite these constraints. 
Conclusion
Aggregate household finances remain resilient: spending and 
savings both increased in Q3 and mortgage buffers continued 
to rebuild. Discretionary spending has also held up despite the 
sharp increase in fuel costs.

But aggregate resilience masks a much more uneven 
distribution of pressure. Low-buffer households 
simultaneously face higher mortgage repayments, greater fuel 
exposure and much smaller savings cushions. While these 
households have so far maintained discretionary spending, 
their capacity to absorb further pressure is clearly more 
limited.
About the Westpac-DataX Consumer Panel
Developed by DataX, Westpac’s data analytics service for 
institutional and business banking customers, the dataset links 
transaction activity with balance sheet information to give 
a complete view of income, spending, saving and borrowing 
flows. 
The resulting sample of over one million customers provides 
an accurate representation of trends across the wider 
Australian consumer. Monthly observations are available back 
to January 2019 with timely updates provided a few weeks 
after the end of each month. As such, it is perfectly suited to 
quickly tracing the impact and responses to events like interest 
rate moves and energy price shocks.

## Document 354: G10 FX Outlook 2027_ It’s hard to look beyond the Fed

Source PDF: all report pdf/G10 FX Outlook 2027_ It’s hard to look beyond the Fed.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-12
Pages: 17
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

In a very uncertain world, this year’s more hawkish steer from
the Federal Reserve has provided a constant and supportive
anchor for the dollar. While the immediate picture for the dollar
looks quite constructive, a turn in the inﬂation cycle by the
second half of 2027 should usher in a modest downtrend

Chris Turner and Francesco Pesole
Executive summary
We all know that the ‘big dollar’ call sets the tone for global
markets – and that call is largely inﬂuenced by the Fed. What
seems clear as we go to press is that the central bank is not about
to turn dovish. In fact, inﬂuential centrist Christopher Waller has just
made his case for opening the door to a 50bp hike this December.
That hawkish, dollar-positive tilt stands in contrast to the typically
negative seasonal factors faced by the dollar in December. This
year, however, the dollar has enough positives to defy that pattern.
US growth might slow slightly from the 3.4% quarter-on-quarter
annualised rate in the third quarter but remains strong. And what

### Page 2

seems to be a strengthening jobs market and inﬂation way above
target look set to keep tightening expectations ﬁrm for a couple of
months yet. That probably means the low-yielding yen and Swiss
franc can remain on the back foot over the coming months.
It seems a diﬃcult call to make today, but the ING house view
forecasts just one 25bp Fed hike in December to conclude this
recalibration of Fed policy. We are not forecasting an AI bust but are
positioning ourselves at the dovish end of the Fed spectrum for

2027. Here, further pressure on the housing market, alongside
lower energy prices and softer wages should give the Fed enough
room to ease restrictive policy in the ﬁnal months of 2027. Such an
outcome feels a distant prospect right now.
An ‘up then down’ dollar baseline view points to relatively conﬁned
ranges in 2027. But in keeping with the theme of this outlook, ‘Fat
tails, slim ranges’, extreme scenarios need to be considered. We
look at those risks in more detail in other articles in this publication.

Beyond the reaction functions of the Fed and the European Central
Bank, the two most important drivers of EUR/USD will likely be the
path of the AI story and the geopolitical/energy scenario.
We look at the range of possible EUR/USD outcomes in light of those
risks below. As we learned last year, pushing a baseline view too
heavily without suﬃcient regard to foreign policy possibilities can
prove dangerous.

EUR/USD: Hope for the best, prepare for the

Fed stays hawkish through the ﬁrst quarter of 2027: Underpinning
dollar strength through the second half of this year has been the
hawkish Federal Reserve. New Chair Kevin Warsh’s commitment to

restore monetary credibility has put paid to dollar debasement fears.
We look for hawkish Fed pricing to remain the dominant dollar theme
through the ﬁrst quarter of 2027, driven by a solid US labour market,
inﬂation still in the 3.5-4.0% area and AI investment still contributing to
growth. Our baseline sees the dollar turning lower in the second
quarter, when inﬂation will be falling back to 2.0% and money markets
will be questioning whether the Fed needs to keep policy at 100bp

above neutral with inﬂation back at target. This assumes one Fed hike
this December to 4.25% and that the Fed is able to cut rates at the end
of 2027.
ECB to deliver ﬁnal hike in December: The eurozone economy has put
in a resilient performance this year despite higher energy prices. The
European Central Bank (ECB) has already hiked rates twice this year and
is expected to hike to 2.75% in December. That should be enough to
contain inﬂation, which is still largely driven by energy. Second-round

eﬀects are nowhere to be seen. Our team sees eurozone growth slowly

### Page 4

picking up through 2027 to deliver full-year growth of 1.2%. Like the
Fed, we think the ECB will have room to ease restrictive monetary
policy towards the end of 2027. There will also be a focus on who
succeeds ECB President Christine Lagarde by next February. Klaas Knot
and Pablo Hernández de Cos have made it to the ﬁnal round. A sea
change in ECB policy looks unlikely, given that a new chief economist
will balance whoever gets the top job.
EUR/USD baseline and risks: Speculators are well on their way to

building extensive short euro positions. The data suggests the European
buy-side, just as was the case in early 2025, is prepared to hold
positions in US assets with very low FX hedge ratios. Our base case
assumes continued trading in a 1.10-1.15 range over the next four to
ﬁve months and then a modest rebound towards 1.18 by end-2027 as
US inﬂation drops and the Fed loosens restrictive policy. At a time of a
resilient US economy, a lower EUR/USD is probably the more vulnerable

side over the coming months. This could potentially be driven by a
large spike in energy prices or the French budgetary situation curtailing
any further ECB hikes and, in an extreme case, triggering ECB bond
market intervention. The most pressing upside EUR/USD risk is probably
a much deeper stock market correction, with US consumers hit by
wealth eﬀects and the market swinging towards Fed cuts, not hikes.
USD/JPY: Friends in high places
Spot Year ahead bias 4Q26 1Q27 2Q27 3Q

USD/JPY 160.00 Bearish 158.00 158.00 155.00 15
160 remains the line in the sand: Ever since Washington joined Tokyo’s
defence of the yen in late July, USD/JPY has not been able to hold a
move above 160. The ﬁrst joint intervention to support the yen since
1998 sent a strong signal of aligned interests. Japan does not want a
weaker yen to make the cost of living any worse. Washington does not
want a higher USD/JPY to drag the USD/Asia complex with it,

undermining eﬀorts to level the playing ﬁeld for US manufacturers.
Even though the US Treasury only intervened in small amounts of
EUR/JPY at the time, the commitment to keep USD/JPY under 160 looks
clear. A hawkish Fed makes that task harder, but the Bank of Japan (BoJ)
should be closing the rate gap. We look for a hike to 1.50% in December
and then another in March. A pronounced turn lower in USD/JPY may
not emerge until the second quarter of next year, however, when we

have short-dated US rates ﬁnally starting to drop.

### Page 5

Takaichi tries to reﬂate the economy: Prime Minister Sanae Takaichi’s
policies are focused on building strength both domestically and
internationally. Domestically, her party, the Liberal Democratic Party
(LDP), is pursuing a strategic JPY370tr investment target by 2040,
utilising both public and private money, targeted across 17 strategic
sectors. Growing at around 0.8%, the Japanese economy is already
around trend output, and Japan may be one of the few G10 economies

with a positive output gap in 2027. This means the BoJ could be one of
the central banks to hike more than expected or be among the last to
cut rates in a global downtrend. This backdrop is keeping the BoJ
hawkish and the focus on whether corporate proﬁts are still driving
wages higher. Recent evidence suggests they are, which is one of the
reasons why the BoJ has recently concluded that its 2% inﬂation target
has been stably met. A key focus in 2027 will be whether Japan can use

productive investment to oﬀset its labour shortage challenges.
Yen remains cheap: Driven by years of low inﬂation and nominal
depreciation, the real trade-weighted yen remains exceptionally weak.
We estimate that USD/JPY is around 20% overvalued on medium-term
fundamentals, with further yen downside now protected by the Tokyo-
Washington alliance. Our call for 152 next year is largely based on the
dollar decline from spring next year. The surprise package, however,

could be Japan’s domestic story, were the economy to start beneﬁting
more broadly from the AI buildout. Here, Japan’s role is supplying the
hardware, manufacturing technology and infrastructure. We have seen
in Korea this year how the AI export story has broadened into stronger
domestic demand and a more hawkish central bank. Higher growth
rates domestically could be an important piece of the jigsaw that
ﬁnally brings home a more substantial part of Japanese earnings

retained overseas.
GBP/USD: Another year of tight ranges
Spot Year ahead bias 4Q26 1Q27 2Q27 3Q27 4
GBP/USD 1.33 Mildly Bullish 1.36 1.36 1.38 1.38 1
BoE matches Fed hawkishness: One abiding feature of core money
markets this year was how readily traders were prepared to price in
Bank of England (BoE) hikes. Sterling rates did have one of the highest
correlations with oil prices, but even when Brent corrected below
$100/bbl recently, markets still held onto views of a terminal BoE rate

of near 4.75%. Important for the GBP/USD story is the timing of the
relative US and UK inﬂation trajectories in 2027. Our team sees US

### Page 6

inﬂation coming back towards 2% in the second quarter as energy base
eﬀects drop out. In the UK, however, the lagged implementation of the
energy price caps means that inﬂation does not start to turn lower until
the third quarter. Our call is therefore that US rates could turn lower
before those in the UK, and that GBP/USD could be retesting the 1.37-
1.38 range highs in the second quarter.
Some creeping optimism: It might not seem like it given that GBP/USD

is still trapped in the middle of its current 1.32-1.37 range, but investors
are getting more positive on the pound. The price of the one-year 25-
delta risk reversal, which measures the price of a call option over a put
option, is now showing the smallest skew towards GBP puts since March.
That has not shown up in the FX positioning data yet, where asset
managers are still running large sterling shorts by historical standards.
Buy-side surveys also show international fund managers are still

substantially underweight in UK equities. But positioning does present
an upside risk to GBP/USD as we head into year-end. If it were to break
higher, we suspect it might be driven more by surprise weakness in the
dollar, however. This is because the BoE tightening cycle into next year
looks fully priced.
Fiscal beneﬁts: In a world where ﬁscal trajectories are being
increasingly scrutinised, the UK falls on the more encouraging side of
the ledger. At least the direction of travel looks encouraging for the

budget deﬁcit, while the fact that the UK has a ﬁscal rule, unlike the US,
is welcome. We also note that the UK has quite a healthy duration on
its government debt stock at 13.5 years, compared with 5.9 years for
the US. The UK government’s reﬁnancing requirements as a percentage
of GDP are much smaller at 8.7%, according to the IMF, compared with
30.8% for the US. Whether sterling would outperform the dollar in a
major bond market sell-oﬀ remains to be seen, but the UK’s debt

maturity proﬁle and near-term debt ﬁnancing needs certainly look
better than those in the US.
EUR/JPY: Steady, with downside risks
Spot Year ahead bias 4Q26 1Q27 2Q27 3Q
EUR/JPY 177.00 Neutral 179.00 179.00 180.00 17
Positive correlation with growth and equities: Both Europe and Japan
have faced their struggles with high energy prices, but both economies
have performed quite well. Europe has beneﬁted from the growing
ﬁscal stimulus put in place last year, which is starting to show through

### Page 7

in series like German manufacturing orders. Japan’s economy has been
protected by the government’s use of energy stockpiles, and in 2027,
Japanese consumers will beneﬁt from a large cut in VAT on food next
April. Yet EUR/JPY remains positively correlated with growth and pro-risk
sentiment in general and would suﬀer in an equity downturn. Decent
advances in corporate proﬁts suggest that a large equity correction is
not a given, while forecasts for continued growth and a turn in inﬂation

in the second half of 2027 make the case for continued equity gains
and a supported EUR/JPY.
Focus on France: Japanese investors have historically been big
investors in French sovereign bond markets, although these ﬂows seem
to have stalled over recent years. French debt had been seen as a
higher-yielding, liquid alternative to German Bunds. However, France’s
unresolved budgetary situation and the threat of further sovereign
downgrades urge caution. Next up is the S&P rating review on 27

November. Our base case is that the current French government passes
a 2027 budget by decree and that some modest ﬁscal consolidation is
enough to avoid dramatic downgrades. This is no 2011-2012 situation,
when the market was shut to Greek sovereign issuance. Were we to be
underestimating the pressure on French debt, the solution would be
euro-negative, especially if the ECB needed to use its Transmission
Protection Instrument, which would eﬀectively mean restarting

quantitative easing. Additionally, some slight narrowing in eurozone-
Japan interest rates might encourage Japanese investors to increase
hedge ratios on euro-denominated debt holdings, which would be a
EUR/JPY negative.
Yen or euro the preferred funding currency? Inevitably, there will be
periods in 2027 when cross-market volatility declines and investors
again return to the carry trade. But what will the preferred funding
currency be in the G10 space? The Swiss franc has the lowest borrowing

rates, but Japanese rates should be closing in on euro rates by the end
of 2027. At that point, we could be looking at the BoJ policy rate near
1.75-2.00% and the ECB rate at 2.50%. Unless eurozone growth sharply
surprises on the upside and there is a substantial rotation into euro-
denominated asset markets, we suspect investors will be considering
euro-funded carry trades given the nature of European political risk in
2027. Assuming inﬂation does turn lower through 2027, we expect the

ECB to be more welcoming of a weaker euro than Japan is of a weaker
yen. Overall, we see a relatively ﬂat EUR/JPY through 2027 but, again,
with greater risk to the downside.
EUR/GBP: Let’s not get carried away

Spot Year ahead bias 4Q26 1Q27 2Q27 3Q27 4
EUR/GBP 0.84 Mildly Bullish 0.83 0.83 0.84 0.85
Sterling has overshot rate diﬀerentials: EUR/GBP is already trading
substantially lower than interest rate diﬀerentials would suggest. Some
of that is probably down to a risk premium in the euro on the back of
French budgetary risk. Sterling may also be playing a limited role as a
safe haven at the moment, given it trades at lower volatility than many
G10 FX pairs, oﬀers high interest rates and has a powerful ﬁscal rule,

which means that in 2027 the UK budget deﬁcit should narrow to close
to 3% of GDP. At the same time, we think ongoing merger and
acquisition inﬂows to the UK are playing a role here too. £141bn of deal
ﬂows have already been completed year-to-date, and there’s another
£260bn of pending or proposed inﬂows. That said, historic correlations
with yield spreads suggest EUR/GBP should be trading closer to 0.86,
meaning that current levels are stretched.

Low volatility to continue: The current analyst consensus for EUR/GBP
is 0.86 for end-2027, with a range of forecasts stretching from 0.82 to
0.91. One of the biggest trends in this pair over recent years has been
the structural decline in volatility following the political turbulence
surrounding Brexit. One-year implied volatility is trading below 5%, with
realised volatility trading well below 4%. That low volatility and the
relatively high cost of hedging are presumably keeping hedge ratios low

on sterling receivables. Assuming that the eurozone and UK economies
move broadly in line next year, we see both growing at around 1.2%
and doubt volatility needs to pick up markedly. The key threat to this
benign environment is probably political, where risks could emerge
from both sides of the Channel.
Unwinding Brexit? One wild card for the pound is the risk that Prime
Minister Andy Burnham opts for early elections next year. Labour is
performing well in the polls, and long-term government objectives on

social care and getting closer to Europe look like they will require a
mandate from the British electorate. How close the Labour Party wants
to get to Europe remains to be seen. At present, Labour policy wants
merely to start a conversation on the subject. But were Labour to put
another referendum in its election manifesto, sterling would take a lot
more positive notice. That said, an early election might not be a one-
sided risk for sterling if, say, the ﬁscal rule were to come up for

renegotiation.

Spot Year ahead bias 4Q26 1Q27 2Q27 3Q27
EUR/CHF 0.92 Mildly Bearish 0.91 0.91 0.90 0.90
Franc plays important role in SNB policy: Having proved a popular
funding currency during the summer, the rebound in the franc and the
drop in EUR/CHF have been driven by lower core rates and the re-
emergence of French budgetary risk. So far, the Swiss National Bank
(SNB) has sounded happy to keep the policy rate at zero even while the
rest of the world is tightening. Our house call remains for the SNB to

keep rates unchanged, and we doubt it would tighten even if inﬂation
nudged above 2% for a couple of months. However, much lower
inﬂation in Switzerland than in its trading partners means that the real
franc has been weakening. The SNB does not want the extra monetary
stimulus of a weaker real exchange rate right now, and we suspect it
will be happy with some nominal franc appreciation. We are not calling
for a repeat of the FX selling by the SNB in 2022-2023 but saying that

another run at 0.90 in EUR/CHF may actually suit the central bank.
Less concern over a strong franc: The minutes from the most recent
SNB meeting in September reported that companies were mentioning
the Swiss franc a lot less in their conversations with central bank
agents. In the past, the SNB had come under a lot of pressure from the
corporate sector over a strong franc, but these concerns seem to have
eased. This may be a function of the strong Swiss economy this year,

where growth is expected to come in at 1.5-2.0% this year and 1.5%
next year. The pharma sector has been a big contributor here and is
seen exporting around $115bn in 2026. Our sector experts expect a
compound annual growth rate (CAGR) of 4.5% until 2030, helped in part
by biologics. Also helping the sector are the exemptions from the 12.5-
15% US tariﬀs on the sector struck by major Swiss pharma companies
in return for investment in US manufacturing.

A preferred safe haven: The franc strengthened its credentials as a safe
haven during both 2025 and 2026. A solid AAA-rated issuer, twin
surpluses and a net foreign asset position worth over 100% of GDP
make the franc a go-to choice in the event of either a bond sell-oﬀ or
European political risk, both of which are likely to be present in 2027. At
the same time, the SNB has backed away from its "increased
willingness to intervene", a phrase introduced when EUR/CHF brieﬂy

broke below 0.90 in March. In reality, that threat had overstated the
SNB’s intentions, given that it only bought CHF4bn in the ﬁrst quarter
and under CHF2bn in the second. We therefore expect EUR/CHF to stay

EUR/SEK: Gliding back to pre-war levels
Spot Year ahead bias 4Q26 1Q27 2Q27 3Q
EUR/SEK 11.05 Mildly Bearish 11.00 10.90 10.90 10
Riksbank’s tightening cycle should be limited: The Riksbank has clearly
communicated that its baseline is for rates to reach 2.25-2.50% from
the current 2.0% by mid-2027. We agree, and while the risks remain
ﬁnely balanced between one and two more hikes, we are sticking to our

call for just one in February or March after the latest inﬂation numbers
materially undershot expectations. Still, our forecasts point to some
upside risk to the Riksbank’s 2027 inﬂation projections, with CPIF
excluding energy and temporary ﬁscal measures - the central bank’s
favoured measure - potentially peaking at 2.5-2.7% in 2Q, compared
with its 2.3% baseline. Combined with a still-solid Swedish growth
outlook, that makes our Riksbank call one of the most exposed to

hawkish revisions in the coming months. Importantly, we don’t expect
conditions for a rate cut to emerge before 2028 and estimate the long-
run neutral rate at 2.25%.
Medium- and short-term factors point to a moderate EUR/SEK
decline: Rate diﬀerentials have been the name of the game recently for
EUR/SEK. In line with this FX Outlook’s call, we think this will remain the
case next year and prove a net negative for the pair. The two-year
overnight index swap (OIS) rate gap is currently around 40bp, down

from a peak of around 60bp in September. We expect the gap to narrow
by 15-30bp over the course of 2027. The range reﬂects whether the
Riksbank hikes once or twice, as well as markets switching more quickly
to pricing in ECB rate cuts. We expect one last ECB hike in December
2026 and a cut in the fourth quarter of 2027. The growth diﬀerential
should also remain moderately negative for EUR/SEK. We estimate
2027 growth at 2.1% in Sweden versus 1.2% in the eurozone. That

should favour a gradual return to pre-war ranges in the pair.
Risks to our baseline: We see EUR/SEK stabilising around 11.00 into
end-2026, before gradually returning to 10.80 in 2027. Risks of a bigger
decline stem more from the euro side, as a busy eurozone political
calendar could drive further widening in European government bond
spreads and, in turn, looser ECB policy. Upside risks are mostly
geopolitical. Our baseline assumes Brent returns to $70-80/bbl in 2027,

a clearly optimistic scenario. SEK is less exposed to higher oil prices

EUR/NOK: A year for stabilisation
Spot Year ahead bias 4Q26 1Q27 2Q27 3Q27
EUR/NOK 10.59 Neutral 10.65 10.70 10.70 10.80
Cuts to start in 2H27: We think the bar for another Norges Bank hike is
fairly high. While Norway had an inﬂation problem before the Iran war,
policy was already restrictive at the start of 2026. Policymakers aren’t

ruling out another move but have sounded content with the current
4.5% rate and are mindful of overtightening risks. We broadly agree
with Norges Bank’s latest projections, which see headline and
underlying (ATE) inﬂation falling steadily below 3.0% from 1Q27 and
stabilising around 2.3-2.8% in the second half of next year. Restrictive
policy even before the energy shock, alongside krone appreciation,
limits the risk of further inﬂation overshooting. Policymakers will be

wary of jumping the gun on easing, but a roughly 2% positive real rate
will argue for some unwinding of tightening in 2H27. We forecast two
25bp cuts, in 3Q and 4Q, taking rates to 4.0%. Some growth softening
should contribute to the dovish shift: we expect 1.2% growth in 2027,
with downside risks.
A quieter year for NOK: Markets aren’t fully pricing in rate cuts in
Norway before 2028, so we expect a dovish repricing to weigh on the
krone. At the same time, we doubt Norges Bank will signal a rapid

cutting cycle ahead, as inﬂation will still be above the 2% target in
2027. Incidentally, our view leans on the dovish side of consensus, and
the risks are skewed towards a delay in easing plans. In the ﬁrst half of
the year, anyway, NOK will continue to enjoy a very attractive carry. If
our call for low volatility materialises, carry should limit the size of any
EUR/NOK rally fuelled by lower energy prices. As we see the ECB shift to
easing from 4Q27, the front-end rate diﬀerential in EUR/NOK should turn

only moderately more supportive for the pair. Expectations for lower

Geopolitics and equities the main risks: While our baseline is for a
quiet year for EUR/NOK, the krone is the least liquid currency in G10,
leaving it highly exposed to violent corrections in equities, like those
seen in autumn 2026. Geopolitical developments will remain a key

EUR/DKK: Moderately stronger for longer
Spot Year ahead bias 4Q26 1Q27 2Q27 3Q27 4Q27
EUR/DKK 7.48 Neutral 7.47 7.47 7.47 7.47 7.46
Greater tolerance for EUR/DKK upside: Danmarks Nationalbank (DN)
has signalled greater tolerance for EUR/DKK appreciation this year.
Between October 2019 and January 2020, before the Covid impact,
levels around 7.470-7.475 prompted DKK17.8bn of FX sales to defend
the krone. Similar levels have prevailed throughout this year, with peaks

above 7.476, yet intervention has been much smaller at DKK6.3bn,
spread between June and August. The deviations from the 7.460 peg
target remain very small, reaching around 0.22% at the intraday peak
in July. Still, the signal points to a partial change in the central bank’s
tolerance for EUR/DKK upside drifts.
Intervention to remain ﬁrst line of defence: For some time, we sensed
that contained FX intervention might have been a prelude to larger DN

rate increases versus the ECB, providing structural support to the krone.
Instead, the bar for a rate reaction by DN seems to have been set
higher. Incidentally, even the small FX sales seen this year have been
enough to take the edge oﬀ EUR/DKK, indicating good eﬀectiveness.
Denmark's FX reserves have plateaued in USD terms in 2026, but at
around $110bn (approximately 22% of GDP), they still provide very
large ammunition to protect the DKK peg. We therefore expect FX

Upward drift can linger for longer: In our view, the +40bp policy rate

USD/CAD: A decent outlook for the loonie
Spot Year ahead bias 4Q26 1Q27 2Q27 3Q27 4Q27
USD/CAD 1.42 Bearish 1.41 1.39 1.37 1.35 1.34
BoC to remain cautious: We expect only one more Bank of Canada
(BoC) hike, likely in 1Q27, taking the policy rate to 2.75%. We admit

there are risks of an additional increase to 3.0%, but for now we view
rates at that level as unlikely. Core inﬂation has remained anchored at
2.0% since the start of the Iran war, despite US tariﬀs. The risk of
second-round eﬀects has increased, as lagged supply chain inﬂation
can start to feed through in early 2027, but some factors should keep
these eﬀects relatively contained in our view. Unemployment has
edged lower in 2026, but this reﬂects a further decline in the

participation rate rather than employment strength, with net
employment modestly contracting. Businesses continue to report
muted hiring conditions and no major pickup in expected wage growth,
which has incidentally fallen in 2026 according to oﬃcial data. Trade-
related uncertainty is also weighing on sentiment, while growth
momentum should slow in the new year, limiting businesses' ability to
pass through higher producer prices.
Front-end to favour USD/CAD softening: We expect the Fed to start

cutting rates in late 2027, dragging front-end USD rates markedly lower.
CAD short-term rates are the most correlated with the US in G10, which
often means the loonie lags most other peers during a dovish Fed
repricing. Still, BoC expectations are not aggressive, with markets
currently embedding another 50bp of tightening over the next year,
similar to the USD curve. Crucially, we estimate Fed rates are currently

around 75bp above neutral, broadly in line with FOMC dot plot

USD/CAD 2yr swap rate gap to retighten to pre-war levels, warranting a

Trump remains the big risk for CAD: Markets have largely dismissed the
escalation in the US-Canada trade spat in 2026, with the negative
impact on CAD remaining very contained. The US midterm result may
suggest that the widely unpopular trade war with Canada will be
shelved, increasing the chance of a comprehensive deal ahead of the
yearly United States-Mexico-Canada Agreement (USMCA) review due in
July 2027. Our baseline is for a new USMCA deal to be agreed this year,

although negotiations may be complicated and potentially involve
threats of new US tariﬀs on Canada. This may make for a bumpy path
towards an eventual CAD-positive outcome. Elsewhere, CAD may
emerge as a commodity currency of choice should geopolitical or trade
risks ﬂare up in Europe or Asia. Conversely, the risk of an AI bust could
hit CAD more than its European peers in our view, given the Canadian
economy's exposure to US activity.
AUD/USD: RBA cuts to limit upside

Spot Year ahead bias 4Q26 1Q27 2Q27 3Q27 4
AUD/USD 0.68 Mildly Bullish 0.69 0.69 0.70 0.70
RBA to shift to cuts in 2H27: We think the Reserve Bank of Australia
(RBA) is done with hikes after having taken the policy rate to 4.85%, the
highest in the G10. While policymakers haven’t entirely shut the door to
further tightening, the bar looks high as growth and housing concerns
strengthen the dovish case. Restrictive ﬁnancial conditions, housing
weakness and softer conﬁdence should weigh on domestic demand,

while data centre investment is unlikely to provide a suﬃcient oﬀset.
We expect unemployment to reach 4.8-5.0% in 2027 and rise further in
2028. We think that will lead to a shift to easing earlier than most other
central banks, with 25bp cuts in both 3Q27 and 4Q27. That would be the
start of a substantial easing cycle in our view, leading to a terminal rate
of 3.60% by end-2028. While the Australian dollar is likely to start the
year with some of the best risk-adjusted carry in the G10, its rate

attractiveness should deteriorate throughout the year.

Fundamentals favour stable outlook: We forecast the rate impact to be
a modest net-positive for AUD/USD, though. Markets are not pricing in
any extra tightening by the RBA, while already factoring in a cut by
end-2027. Instead, the USD curve is far more mispriced on the hawkish
side, in our view. We expect a roughly +20bp move in the AUD/USD
short-term rate diﬀerential over the next 12 months, which is
consistent with a sustainable return to 0.71 by the end of the year.

However, we expect AUD to lag many G10 peers, especially in the
second half of the year when RBA cuts will dent its carry attractiveness.
We also see Australia’s terms of trade moderating lower, in line with our
view for lower oil, natural gas and iron ore prices. On the latter, our
commodities team forecasts an average of $90-95/t on the back of new
supply from West Africa hitting the market.
AI the biggest risk for AUD: The Aussie dollar’s main vulnerability is

undoubtedly the external risk environment, especially the threat of a
tech-led equity selloﬀ. Recent episodes have consistently seen AUD
underperform most other G10 currencies, and we expect its high
implied yield to keep the Aussie a high-beta currency throughout next
year. We therefore see less scope for AUD to beneﬁt from idiosyncratic
US asset underperformance, particularly compared with low yielders.
The Aussie also remains exposed to any deterioration in China-related

sentiment. While our baseline view for China is growth stabilisation, any
renewed Sino-America trade tensions or geopolitical risk emerging in
Asia could trigger a material proxy-response by AUD.

Two more hikes by RBNZ: The Reserve Bank of New Zealand (RBNZ)
surprised with a split-vote hold in October, when markets and
consensus were fully aligned for a hike. Half of the committee holds a

rather pessimistic view on domestic and international growth prospects
and will be wary of overtightening. That view was partly validated by a
soft 3Q jobs market report. That said, the inﬂation picture continues to
argue for higher rates, in our view. We see upside risks to the RBNZ’s
latest inﬂation projections that show headline inﬂation gliding back to
2.1% by end-2027. The Bank’s projections already embed one hike from
the current 2.75% and split chances of another afterwards. Our call is

for a hike in December and another in March, reaching a 3.25%
terminal rate. The risks are skewed to a later resumption of hikes, but

Read more
we think a material downward surprise in growth would be required to
prevent at least two hikes. Importantly, we don’t currently expect any

cuts by the RBNZ before 1Q28.
Rate support should improve for NZD: We expect a material decline in
USD front-end rates. The Kiwi dollar’s swap curve is also more hawkish
than our expectations, embedding 3.50% for one year ahead and even
another hike two years ahead. At some point in 2027, we think market
expectations for the RBNZ will be revised lower and will start to price in
a long-term neutral rate around 3.0-3.25%. Still, there should be a
material improvement in the short-term rate diﬀerential for NZD/USD,

which should drive spot back to 0.60 next year, in our estimates. The
starting point is incidentally one of short-term undervaluation, with the
pair’s recent selloﬀ larger than warranted by market fundamentals.
NZD would remain a low-yielding alternative to AUD, therefore limiting
idiosyncratic strength, but the gap should start closing as we see
markets pricing in a substantial RBA rate-cutting cycle from 2H27, while
the RBNZ’s lower starting point should curb dovish speculation. We

expect a return to the 1.15-1.20 range in AUD/NZD.
Double exposure to geopolitics and AI: The Kiwi dollar will remain
vulnerable to escalations in the Middle East due to its net-negative
exposure to higher energy prices. Any deterioration in China-related
sentiment remains another risk, although we’d expect a slightly smaller
impact on NZD than AUD, given Australia’s higher dependency on
Chinese demand. We have a similar assessment with regard to an AI-

bust scenario. NZD would be among the worst performers given its high
beta to risk sentiment, but AUD may suﬀer bigger losses as the RBA has
further room to cut in an adverse environment. We think the upside
risks to our NZD baseline forecast mostly stem from the domestic
activity/inﬂation story in New Zealand proving much stronger than
expected, leaving greater room for the RBNZ to tackle elevated
inﬂation with interest rate increases.
